BuildBridge
Core tagline: Bridging traditional learning to real-world work.
Product promise: Move beyond watching videos and collecting course-completion certificates. Learn through building, demonstrate what you can do, and gain experience backed by real work.
Experience principle: Make the journey simple, understandable and connected — all in one place.
Product loop: Learn it. Build it. Prove it. Earn from it.

BuildBridge is an AI-powered learning-to-work platform that helps students move from traditional, mostly content-based learning into practical, evidence-backed experience. It brings existing learning resources together, organises them around students' skills and goals, helps them understand and apply what they learn, and connects that learning to real projects and professional opportunities.
The central idea: Traditional learning often ends when a student finishes a video, course or assessment and receives a completion certificate. BuildBridge is designed to take the next step: help the student use that knowledge to build something real, document the work, receive meaningful feedback or company approval, and earn credentials and experience backed by demonstrated projects and deliverables.

Document purpose: This is the comprehensive developer-handoff README for the current BuildBridge codebase. It records the original product thesis, full product vision, architecture, implemented behaviour, known limitations, observed bugs, planned work and operational instructions so future developers and AI coding sessions can understand the project without relying on chat history. It is not yet the concise, judge-optimised public README. Status labels must be re-verified against the current branch and Production before making claims to judges.

Project links
- GitHub repository: https://github.com/sukumarisadhukhan/BuildBridge
- Latest deployment URL supplied during development: https://build-bridge-nsbsx7kj5-sukumari-sadhukhan.vercel.app/
- Deployment note: The URL above may be a deployment-specific Vercel URL. Confirm the canonical Production domain in Vercel before treating it as the permanent public application URL. Some Vercel deployments may have access protection enabled; verify public/incognito access before sharing with judges.
1. Original problem and product thesis
1.1 The gap in traditional learning
Students can access an enormous amount of educational content: recorded lectures, YouTube videos, online courses, tutorials, documentation, academic syllabi and certificates. These resources are often scattered across separate platforms. Students have to decide what is relevant, what to study first, how to connect one resource to another and how to know whether they can actually apply what they watched.

The deeper problem is not only that resources are scattered. It is that the traditional learning journey can stop at content consumption and course completion. Watching videos, completing modules and collecting certificates may show exposure to a topic, but they do not by themselves prove that a student can apply the knowledge, build a solution, work through technical requirements or deliver something useful to another person or organisation. Students are then expected to find projects, gain experience, build a portfolio and approach employers separately.

BuildBridge was conceived to bridge that gap: from traditional learning to real-world work. It makes existing learning easier to find and understand, then gives students a connected route to apply that learning through projects, build a record of evidence, gain feedback and access relevant professional opportunities.
1.2 What BuildBridge changes
Traditional, disconnected journey	BuildBridge journey
Watch videos and search across many platforms independently.	Discover and organise existing resources in one place, filtered and recommended by skills, goals, experience and knowledge gaps.
Finish a course and receive a completion certificate, with limited evidence of practical application.	Use learning as the starting point for practice, projects and demonstrable deliverables.
Work out alone how academic concepts relate to industry requirements.	Map coursework and career goals to skills, learning resources and practical project challenges.
Find projects and beginner opportunities through scattered websites and unfamiliar technical issue descriptions.	Discover relevant practice, open-source and verified company projects, with guidance to understand requirements.
Keep repositories, reports, screenshots, feedback and certificates in separate places.	Preserve learning records, project work, submissions, reports, feedback and credentials in one Student Learning & Work Vault.
Claim skills based mainly on course completion or self-description.	Distinguish studied, practised, demonstrated and company-verified skills using associated evidence and approvals.
Search separately for experience and professional opportunities.	Connect demonstrated skills and project evidence to relevant internships, freelance assignments, paid micro-projects and other opportunities.


The key shift is from learning about work to learning by building and demonstrating work. BuildBridge does not remove videos, courses or existing learning providers. It organises and uses them as the foundation for practical application.
1.3 The role of certificates and experience
BuildBridge recognises that certificates can represent different kinds of achievement. A course-completion or theory-assessment certificate can record that a student completed learning or demonstrated knowledge in an assessment. It should not automatically be treated as proof of professional experience.

The distinctive direction of BuildBridge is to make stronger credentials and experience traceable to actual work: completed projects, repositories, deployments, reports, deliverables, milestone submissions, feedback and, for company assignments, explicit approval from the assigning verified company. Company completion certificates are therefore backed by company-approved project work and the required authorised signatory process.

BuildBridge may also retain legitimate learning/theory credentials, but it must clearly distinguish them from project-evidence and company-completion credentials. It must never misrepresent a practice project, a course certificate or an AI-generated assessment as employer-approved professional experience.
1.4 One simple, connected place
BuildBridge brings the pieces of the journey together so students do not have to manage learning, projects, proof and opportunities as unrelated activities across disconnected tools. It aims to make the process easy to understand and implement: show the student what to learn, where to find suitable resources, how to apply the knowledge, what evidence to submit and what the next opportunity could be.

BuildBridge is not merely an LMS, job board, recruitment platform, open-source issue list or project-management application. Those capabilities support one central purpose: helping a student turn learning into demonstrable capability and real-world experience.
1.5 The connected learning-to-work journey
1. Discover: Find existing learning resources and practical opportunities relevant to the student.
2. Personalise: Organise resources and next steps around skills, career goals, coursework, experience and knowledge gaps.
3. Learn: Understand concepts through videos, courses, tutorials, documentation, coursework and AI-assisted explanations.
4. Build: Apply knowledge through practice projects, open-source contributions and verified company assignments.
5. Prove: Record repositories, deployments, reports, screenshots, deliverables, submissions, milestones and feedback.
6. Validate: Distinguish self-declared, practised, demonstrated and company-verified capabilities; obtain the appropriate human/company approvals.
7. Grow and earn: Use evidence-backed capabilities to discover relevant internships, freelance assignments, paid micro-projects and other opportunities.
1.6 Core product principles
- Traditional learning is the starting point, not the endpoint. Videos, courses and documentation help students learn; practical projects help them apply what they learn.
- Bridge learning to work. Every relevant learning path should lead towards an application task, project or demonstrable outcome where appropriate.
- Make learning simple and understandable. Students should not need to navigate many disconnected platforms or decode complex requirements without help.
- Organise existing resources rather than recreate every course. Find, sort, explain and present relevant resources from existing providers in one place.
- Personalise by skills and goals. Recommendations should explain why a resource or project is relevant.
- Maintain one persistent development record. Preserve learning history, skills, projects, files, reports, evidence, feedback and credentials.
- Back claims with evidence. A course completion, self-declared skill, practice submission and company-approved deliverable are different forms of evidence and must be labelled accurately.
- Use AI as a guide, not an authority that fabricates achievement. AI suggestions and reviews do not replace required company or administrator approvals.
- Keep practice and industry work distinct. A platform practice project is not an employer assignment.
- Do not promise outcomes that BuildBridge cannot guarantee. Opportunities may state compensation, but selection, stipends, payment and employment are not guaranteed.
1.7 The central differentiator
BuildBridge connects three things that are usually disconnected:
1. Accessible, organised learning: existing resources arranged by skills, goals and experience, with help understanding and applying them.
2. Work-backed proof and experience: a long-term record of projects, repositories, deployments, reports, submissions, milestones, feedback and approvals.
3. A path to real opportunities: matching relevant professional work to demonstrated capabilities and eligibility.
The goal is for a student to answer these questions in one place: What should I learn? Where can I learn it? How do I build something with it? How can I prove I did it? What real opportunity can I pursue next?
2. Product workflows
Students can enter through either of two learning paths. Both converge into the same learning-to-work journey.
2.1 Career-first / discovery path
A student describes a target role, career direction or the kind of work they want to pursue. BuildBridge can recommend career directions, explain trade-offs and construct a skill roadmap.

A career pathway should connect:
- Target role or career goal.
- Skills and prerequisite knowledge.
- Recommended existing resources.
- Suggested order of study.
- Practice opportunities.
- Projects that demonstrate the relevant skills.
- Evidence needed to demonstrate progress.
- Relevant professional opportunities when eligibility is met.
2.2 Coursework-first path
A student provides a syllabus, assignment or supported course material. The application can use Gemini to extract concepts and map them to skills, career relevance and project challenges.

Existing documented behaviour includes:
- PDF or plain-text syllabus/assignment upload (.txt, .md, .csv), with a documented 4 MB limit.
- Gemini-based extraction of structured concepts, mapped skills and project suggestions.
- The original upload is not intended to be retained by the extraction workflow.
- A separate theory knowledge check can be generated from uploaded material.
The coursework-first workflow has had previous Production database/schema-cache errors. Its current end-to-end status must be tested against the live database rather than inferred from old validation reports.
2.3 Skill progression model
BuildBridge distinguishes four skill states:
1. Studied: The student has engaged with a learning resource or coursework covering the skill.
2. Practised: The student has practised or applied the skill in exercises or practice work.
3. Demonstrated: The student has provided project or other accepted evidence showing application of the skill.
4. Company-verified: The assigning verified company has explicitly approved relevant industry work or milestones supporting the skill.
Do not automatically promote a skill to demonstrated or company-verified merely because a student lists it in a profile or completes a course. Preserve the evidence and approval basis for each state.
2.4 Learning resources in one place
BuildBridge should help students discover and organise existing resources such as:
- Courses and modules.
- YouTube videos and playlists.
- Official documentation.
- Tutorials and articles.
- Academic materials and coursework.
- Saved study material and notes.
- External learning resources.
Recommendations should be organised by skill, career goal, experience, knowledge gap and relevance. Each resource should retain its original title, provider and source URL where available.

YouTube recommendations use the YouTube Data API. BuildBridge stores only learning actions that the student records through the application; it cannot access private YouTube watch history. The documented embedded-player completion rule requires at least 90% continuous playback; seeking over material does not count as completed. Opening a video externally records an open event rather than completed learning.
2.5 AI-assisted learning and project guidance
The AI Mentor should help students understand unfamiliar topics, find suitable resources, work through project requirements, debug code, interpret errors, plan implementation and reflect on their work.

The intended mentor behaviour is contextual, progressive and educational:
- Ask clarifying questions when a request is ambiguous.
- Explain concepts at a level suited to the student's experience.
- Give progressive hints instead of immediately replacing the student's work.
- Explain code and technical errors.
- Connect explanations to the student's learning path and active projects.
- Help students understand unfamiliar technical terminology.
- Recommend relevant existing resources.
- Help interpret open-source issues and project acceptance requirements.
- Use prior conversation context appropriately when available.
AI can assist with implementation. Students are assessed on the quality, correctness, validation, integration and delivery of their work, not on avoiding AI.
2.6 Practical project pathways
BuildBridge supports three distinct kinds of project work:
1. BuildBridge Practice Projects: Platform-published learning projects. These help students practise and create portfolio evidence. They are not employer-approved work and do not qualify for company completion certificates.
2. Verified Company Projects: Projects published and managed by verified organisations. Companies define the scope, deliverables, acceptance criteria, deadlines, revision policy and compensation/payment terms where applicable. Only the assigning company can approve its own project's work.
3. External Projects / Open-source Issues: External listings, especially GitHub issues, are clearly separated from internal BuildBridge assignments. External listings do not become BuildBridge projects or imply a partnership with the repository owner.
Students should provide genuine evidence such as:
- Repository URL.
- Deployment URL.
- Screenshots.
- Reports and technical documentation.
- Test evidence.
- Implementation reflection.
- Other required deliverables.
Students cannot simply mark required work complete without the relevant evidence workflow.
2.7 Professional opportunity marketplace
After students demonstrate relevant skills through projects and evidence, BuildBridge should recommend and match:
- Internships.
- Freelance assignments.
- Paid micro-projects.
- Company project opportunities.
- Entry-level and other professional opportunities where supported.
Matching should use demonstrated or company-verified skills, project evidence, career goals, experience and eligibility. Self-declared skills and syllabus topics alone must not be treated as demonstrated capabilities.

Each opportunity should show, where the source provides it:
- Match reasoning.
- Required skills.
- Eligibility and prerequisites.
- Scope and deliverables.
- Deadline.
- Location or work arrangement.
- Compensation/stipend/payment terms.
- Application status.
Match labels are informational. Students must check employer requirements. BuildBridge does not guarantee selection, payment, stipends or employment.
3. Unified Student Learning, Work and Evidence Vault
The Unified Student Vault is a core product capability. It is intended to be the student's persistent repository of learning, practical work, evidence and credentials.

Product label: My Learning & Work Vault
Suggested description: Everything you've learned, built, submitted and achieved, organised in one place.
3.1 What the Vault should contain
Learning resources and history
- Saved courses, videos, tutorials and documentation links.
- Coursework mappings and learning pathways.
- Completed modules and learning activity.
- Notes and supported uploaded learning materials.
- Resource provider, original source and completion history.
Skills and competencies
- Acquired skills and mastery progression.
- Associated learning resources.
- Practice records.
- Projects demonstrating each skill.
- Evidence-backed and company-verified skills.
Projects and practical work
- BuildBridge Practice Projects.
- Coursework projects.
- Open-source contributions.
- Company assignments.
- GitHub repositories and deployment links.
- Project reports, documentation and screenshots.
Professional evidence
- Submissions and deliverables.
- Milestone records.
- Company feedback.
- Revision history.
- Approval records.
- Completion dates and compensation information where relevant.
Reports and documents
- Project completion reports.
- Learning progress reports.
- Technical documentation.
- PDFs and other permitted uploaded files.
- Screenshots, presentations and reflections.
- Company review records.
Certificates and credentials
- BuildBridge learning certificates.
- Skill-demonstration credentials.
- Company project completion certificates.
- Other legitimately issued credentials.
Each certificate should include appropriate metadata such as issuer, certificate type, associated project/achievement, issue date, certificate ID and verification URL where available.
3.2 Vault interface requirements
The Vault should support:
- Search by resource, project, skill or artifact name.
- Category filters.
- Date filters.
- Completion/status filters.
- Organisation filters where applicable.
- Sorting by recent/oldest.
- Document previews where supported.
- Direct links to original resources, GitHub repositories and deployments.
- Download/print for supported files and certificates.
- A chronological learning and achievement timeline.
- Navigation between a skill and its associated resources, projects and evidence.
- Navigation from a project to its skills, submissions, milestones, reviews and certificates.
3.3 Vault architecture and security
The Vault implementation was reported as completed in code by a previous Copilot task. That task reported:
- Supabase artifact persistence.
- Private Storage uploads.
- Role-protected API.
- Searchable/filterable UI.
- Project/evidence/certificate aggregation.
- Chronological timeline.
- Typecheck, lint and production build passed.
However, Production subsequently returned:

Could not find the table 'public.student_vault_artifacts' in the schema cache

This means the Vault must not be described as fully operational in Production until its migration and schema are verified and applied to the correct Supabase project.

The intended design is:
- PostgreSQL for structured metadata and relationships.
- Private Supabase Storage buckets for uploaded files and generated documents.
- External URLs for GitHub repositories, deployments and learning resources.
- Authenticated APIs for reads/writes.
- RLS and Storage policies so students access only their own private material, companies access only their authorised submissions and administrators use governed access.
Each artifact should have appropriate owner, type, title, description, associated skill/project IDs, storage path or external URL, creation/upload date, source/issuer, verification state and related submission/milestone metadata where relevant.

Do not store large files directly in PostgreSQL. Do not copy copyrighted external course content into BuildBridge; save links and permitted metadata instead.
4. Open-source discovery and contribution guidance
4.1 Problem being solved
Students often do not know how open-source contributions work, which repositories suit their current skills, whether an issue is beginner-appropriate, or how to understand unfamiliar technical problem statements.

BuildBridge should make open-source participation less intimidating by combining skill-based discovery with plain-language issue explanations.
4.2 Existing documented external project discovery
The existing README documents:
- GET /api/external-projects requires a signed-in student session.
- Query parameters include q, skill, technology, language, provider, category, sort and page.
- The initial provider is GitHub Search API.
- It requests open, non-pull-request issues labelled good first issue or help wanted.
- Archived repositories are excluded when identifiable.
- Results are normalised with issue and repository URLs.
- Optional server-only GITHUB_TOKEN can improve the authenticated API limit.
- Responses are cached for approximately 30 minutes using Next.js/provider cache headers.
- External results are not inserted into the internal projects table.
- GitHub outages and no-result states are separate from BuildBridge project records.
- Other sources such as Kaggle, internships, freelance and hackathon listings remain authorised-resource links until a provider API or permitted feed is implemented. Do not scrape or fabricate listings.
4.3 Intended skill-based matching
Recommend external projects using the student's:
- Skills and demonstrated skills.
- Interests.
- Career goals.
- Programming languages.
- Experience level.
- Learning goals and prerequisites.
Show why a result matches, which skills are relevant, which prerequisites may be missing and whether it is a likely first-contribution task or a stretch task. Do not invent difficulty or beginner labels that are not supported by source metadata or a clearly labelled heuristic.
4.4 AI issue simplification
For each issue, provide a plain-language explanation covering:
1. What the repository/project does.
2. What problem the issue describes.
3. What contribution is being requested.
4. What unfamiliar terms and technologies mean.
5. Which skills are relevant.
6. A suggested starting approach, clearly identified as guidance rather than maintainer instructions.
Add a functional Show details interaction. Initially show the simplified explanation. On expansion, show the original, unmodified GitHub issue title and body, labels, repository, source link, original requirements/acceptance criteria where present, and other available metadata.

The original issue is the source of truth. AI must not invent requirements or alter the maintainer's instructions.
4.5 Rendering and security
A screenshot showed raw HTML/Markdown from GitHub issue bodies appearing directly in the UI. Fix this using safe Markdown rendering and HTML sanitisation. Block scripts, event handlers and unsafe links. Preserve code blocks and legitimate links.

Treat issue text as untrusted input. Instructions embedded in an external issue must never override BuildBridge's system instructions or the AI Mentor's safety boundary.

If AI simplification fails, display an honest fallback and the original issue rather than a fabricated AI response. Cache summaries appropriately and handle GitHub rate limits.

The AI simplification and Show details workflow is a required product enhancement; verify whether it has been implemented in the latest working tree before marking it complete.
5. The three authenticated workspaces
BuildBridge has separate Student, Company and Administrator workspaces. Role assignment must be enforced server-side.
5.1 Student workspace
Student functionality includes:
- Career discovery and skill roadmap.
- Coursework-first mapping.
- Curated learning resources.
- YouTube recommendations and recorded learning activity.
- AI Mentor.
- Project catalogue and project details.
- Practice and company project submissions.
- External/open-source project discovery.
- Milestone evidence.
- Opportunities and applications.
- Unified Learning & Work Vault.
- Certificates and achievements.
- Student Profile and learning Settings.
The student workflow has been reported as working by the application owner. Preserve it and regression-test it after shared changes.
5.2 Company workspace
Company functionality includes:
- Verified organisation profile.
- Domain/email verification status.
- Project and opportunity publishing.
- Stipend, compensation, scope, eligibility and acceptance terms.
- Student applications.
- Project assignments.
- Milestone management.
- Evidence review.
- Revision requests.
- Explicit company approvals.
- Authorised certificate signatory workflow.
- Company-specific notifications and settings.
Only the assigning verified company can approve its own project. Company project briefs should be locked/snapshotted appropriately after student assignment to protect active work.
5.3 Administrator workspace
The Administrator Console includes:
- Dashboard.
- Company Verification.
- Project Moderation.
- Certificates.
- AI Management.
- Users & Access.
- Disputes.
- Audit Trail.
- Platform Health.
- Settings.
Admin is not selectable during public sign-up. The deployer provisions one trusted admin account manually in Supabase. The existing design uses ADMIN_OWNER_EMAIL and platform_admin_owner; a database unique index enforces the single-admin model.

The administrator is responsible for platform governance, company verification, practice project publishing, authorised platform credential issuance, moderation, access oversight and operational monitoring. Admin must not approve company-assigned project milestones on behalf of the assigning company.
6. Company verification and authorisation
Public sign-up always creates a student profile, regardless of client-provided role metadata. Selecting "Company representative" during onboarding expresses intent; it does not grant company permissions.

Company access and publishing requires the existing trusted workflow:
1. Authenticated user and confirmed email.
2. Appropriate organisation email/domain.
3. Company profile and declared domain.
4. DNS ownership proof.
5. Separate administrator decision.
6. Correct company membership and ownership association.
7. Verified company approval before restricted publishing/approval actions.
The application has existing company/domain infrastructure, including documented references to:
- components/CompanyDomainWorkflow.tsx.
- app/api/company-domain/route.ts.
- companies and company_domain_requests records.
- Existing verification RPCs/migrations.
- Company certificate authorisation functionality.
6.1 Known PostgREST relationship ambiguity
Production has returned:

Could not embed because more than one relationship was found for 'company_domain_requests' and 'profiles'

The actual foreign keys were inspected and confirmed:
- company_domain_requests_reviewed_by_fkey — column reviewed_by → profiles.
- company_domain_requests_user_id_fkey — column user_id → profiles.
Queries must explicitly identify the desired relationship. For example, requester profile joins should use the company_domain_requests_user_id_fkey relationship, and reviewer joins should use company_domain_requests_reviewed_by_fkey. Search all related queries, not just one route. No schema change is needed merely to disambiguate these existing relationships.
6.2 Company approval shortcut in Users & Access
The Users & Access view should show company verification state and, when a matching request exists, a Review Verification action. This should navigate to Company Verification and select the correct organization/request. It must not appear for ordinary students or grant permissions by itself.
7. Project moderation and publishing
7.1 BuildBridge Practice Projects
The Admin Project Moderation screen intentionally contains a Create a BuildBridge Practice Project form. This is a valid administrator function and must not be removed.

Practice projects are original platform-owned learning projects. They can include:
- Title and problem statement.
- Scope.
- Deliverables.
- Acceptance criteria.
- Category and difficulty.
- Project type and duration.
- Required skills.
- Deadline.
- Requirements.
- Milestones.
- Submission format.
- Evaluation rubric.
- Resources.
- Revision policy.
Practice submissions are learning evidence only. They are never employer-approved work and must not be used to issue industry completion certificates.
7.2 Confirmed practice-project draft problem
The application owner reported this Production behaviour:
- Form was filled.
- Save Draft was clicked.
- No success or actionable error message appeared.
- Published and draft records continued to show no project records.
- After leaving and returning, all entered form values were gone.
This is an unresolved persistence issue until verified in Production. Inspect the save handler, validation, authenticated API, correct database table, insert permissions/RLS, listing API and status filtering. A successful save must be confirmed by the database; do not only append the record to local React state. Drafts must remain available after refresh and navigation, and must be reopenable/editable. Publishing is a separate action.
7.3 Company projects
Company projects remain managed by verified publishers. Administrators may oversee policy compliance and reported issues but should not impersonate companies or change active company work without authorised governance procedures.
8. Certificates and credentials
BuildBridge has multiple credential workflows. They must remain distinct.
8.1 Company project completion certificates
A company completion certificate is created only after:
1. The project was assigned by a verified company.
2. The student submitted the required evidence.
3. The assigning company reviewed the work.
4. Every required milestone was explicitly approved by that company.
5. The company owner authorised a named signatory.
The database/company-review workflow is intended to trigger issuance automatically after all conditions are met. The administrator has no approval role in this company completion issuance path.

After issuance:
- Persist the certificate record.
- Add it to the student's credential Vault.
- Make it available in the student dashboard/certificate area.
- Notify the student in-app.
- Send the student an email with certificate access/verification details through the transactional email system.
- Provide a verification URL.
- Prevent duplicate issuance on retries.
The actual Production end-to-end issuance and email-delivery path still requires verification.
8.2 BuildBridge platform credentials
BuildBridge learning, pathway and skill-demonstration certificates are separate platform credentials. These require a platform administrator issuance action supported by recorded evidence.

The Admin Certificates interface shown in the screenshot is for this separate category; it is not the company completion certificate workflow. Keep it, but make the distinction obvious in the UI.
8.3 Theory Learning Certificate
The existing README documents a separate theory knowledge check generated from uploaded coursework material. A student needs at least 80% to receive a BBT-... Theory Learning Certificate. Answers are stored separately and not sent to the browser before submission. Secure quiz creation/grading requires server-side SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY configuration.

Theory Learning Certificates are distinct from project-evidence credentials. The current verification routes include /certificate/verify; public verification should expose only allowed certificate fields, not private account or evidence information.

Legacy certificates from the old workflow are documented as revoked because the old workflow did not require complete-project approval or issuer authorisation.
9. AI Management and platform monitoring
9.1 AI Mentor
The AI Mentor uses Google Gemini through server-side routes. The existing codebase/documentation references app/api/ai/route.ts, @google/genai, GEMINI_API_KEY and GEMINI_MODEL.

The previous mentor response to a simple SQL request was overly generic, assumed MySQL, introduced Node.js/Express without context and displayed a generic menu. This is a confirmed quality/personalisation concern. A response appearing in the UI does not prove it came from a live Gemini generation request.

The Mentor must use relevant authorised student context: career goals, learning paths, coursework, skills, resources, progress, projects and appropriate conversation history. It should explain, guide, give progressive hints, review attempts and connect theory to practical work. It must not fabricate a student's skills or learning history.

Verify a real Gemini request, model compatibility, key validity, error handling, rate limits, timeouts, response parsing and conversation persistence. geminiConfigured: true only proves that a key is present, not that generation works.
9.2 Admin AI Management
The Admin AI Management page was observed displaying the generic empty state:

No records available for this section. Only database-backed records are shown. Platform controls that require a dedicated workflow are not simulated here.

A real AI Control Center is required. It should show actual provider connectivity/generation tests, configured model, last successful request, recent failures, response duration, request success/failure counts and safe operational events where such data is genuinely collected.

Do not generate fake usage numbers or expose private student prompts/conversations, credentials or secrets. Controls must only be displayed when they can genuinely affect configuration; environment-variable changes may require Vercel rather than an in-app editable form.
9.3 Platform Health
/api/health has returned a response showing Supabase configured/reachable and Gemini/YouTube configured. This endpoint checks configuration presence and Supabase reachability; it does not prove that every protected API, AI generation, Storage operation, email delivery or admin action works.

The Platform Health page must distinguish:
- Configuration present.
- Service reachable.
- Actual operation tested successfully.
- Service degraded/unavailable.
Avoid repeated generic Supabase-reachable toast messages when navigating. Health details belong in Platform Health.
10. Audit Trail
The Audit Trail was observed showing No audit events recorded yet.

The documented existing backend endpoint reads recent records from public.audit_logs (up to 100 in the inspected implementation). Existing database logic includes an audit event for company-domain decisions, but event coverage is incomplete or has not been demonstrated.

Implement centralised server-side audit recording for material events:
- Authentication and important access changes.
- Company registration and verification.
- DNS verification and company approval/rejection.
- User-role changes.
- Practice project draft creation, editing, publishing and archival.
- Company project publishing and assignments.
- Evidence submissions and revisions.
- Milestone approvals.
- Certificate issuance and revocation.
- Dispute actions.
- AI/provider incidents.
- Email delivery failures.
- Important platform configuration changes.
Audit records should include actor, role, action, entity type/ID, timestamp, outcome, safe structured metadata and request/correlation ID where practical.

Use the existing audit_logs structure where possible. Make records append-only and administrator-readable under RLS. Do not log passwords, tokens, API keys, private AI conversations or sensitive evidence contents.

Add filters for event category, actor, date and outcome. Do not insert fabricated historical events merely to fill the page.
11. Administrator Settings and Alerts
The Administrator Settings page was observed showing the generic empty-state placeholder. The shared settings UI also previously showed student learning preferences in non-student workspaces.

Admin Settings must include:
- Administrator account information and security.
- Platform appearance/preferences.
- Timezone/date preferences where supported.
- Persistent administrator alert preferences.
- Email delivery and provider configuration status (without exposing secrets).
- AI provider health and database/application status links.
11.1 Administrator alert categories
Company registration and verification
- New company registration.
- New verification request awaiting review.
- DNS verification failure or attention required.
- Company approval/rejection decision.
AI performance
- Gemini provider unavailable.
- Repeated AI generation failures.
- Model unavailable or rate-limited.
- Unusually high response latency.
- AI Mentor conversation/response failures.
Platform errors
- Critical API failures.
- Database connection/query failures.
- Failed project submission.
- Certificate issuance failure.
- Email delivery failure.
- Critical application exception.
The UI preferences must control actual notification behaviour. Use severity levels, deduplication and cooldowns to prevent email storms. Critical security/database/provider outages should receive priority alerts.
12. Email and notification infrastructure
Authentication emails have been observed arriving. Actual BuildBridge application-triggered transactional emails have not yet been reliably demonstrated by the application owner.

Authentication emails and application notification emails are separate systems.

The existing README documents:
- In-app notifications.
- Per-user preferences in Supabase.
- Resend integration.
- An outbox worker at /api/cron/notifications.
- Signed unsubscribe links.
- Scheduled deadline reminders.
- Server-only email secrets.
Inspect the actual current code, cron configuration, outbox, Resend integration, sender verification and environment variable names before changing anything. Reuse existing infrastructure rather than creating a duplicate provider/outbox.
12.1 Email categories
Student: project submission, revision request, milestone approval, certificate issued, relevant opportunity updates.
Company: eligible application, new project submission, review reminder, resubmission, verification decision.
Administrator: new company registration, pending verification, DNS failure, AI incident, database/application failure, certificate issuance failure, email delivery failure.

Requirements:
- Real server-side provider delivery.
- Branded responsive templates.
- Queued/sent/delivered/failed state where provider supports it.
- Safe retry of transient failures.
- Deduplication and rate limiting.
- Respect notification preferences.
- Record and surface delivery failures.
- Never claim an email was sent merely because an in-app notification record exists.
If provider credentials or verified sender identity are missing, document the exact environment variables/settings required. Never invent credentials.
13. Landing page and product communication
The landing page should explain BuildBridge in its first visible sentence.

Recommended headline direction:

Everything you need to learn, build and become work-ready. In one place.

Supporting statement:

"Discover the right learning resources, build practical skills through real projects, document your achievements and connect with relevant professional opportunities."

The product should be communicated through the journey:

Discover → Learn → Build → Prove → Earn

Preserve the current brand language:
- Exact supplied BuildBridge logo artwork.
- Light peach application canvas.
- Warm ivory cards.
- Deep navy typography/navigation.
- Mint/teal actions.
- Violet secondary accents.
- Responsive layouts and readable typography.
Audit every landing page button, CTA, link and navigation element. Remove redundant controls and fix non-functional buttons. Each CTA must navigate to a valid route, scroll to an actual section or perform an actual action. Do not leave buttons that look interactive but do nothing.

The landing page, sign-in pages and authenticated workspace should remain distinct routes.
14. Notifications, settings and user experience
Settings must be role-specific.

Student settings: recommendation language, learning personalization, student project/review updates, deadlines, opportunities and learning notifications.

Company settings: organization preferences, project submissions, milestone reviews, applicants, opportunity activity, deadlines and company verification updates.

Administrator settings: platform alerts, company verification alerts, AI/backend incidents, security and operational notifications.

Preferences must persist and control the actual delivery workflow. Do not show controls for notifications that are not implemented.

The authenticated workspace has a six-step visual walkthrough with distinct illustrations for career direction, coursework mapping, goal-matched video learning, project building, evidence submission and company approval. The existing README documents a persistent "Your Build Journey" guide at the top of workspace pages, automatic advancement every four seconds (unless reduced motion is enabled), pause/resume, step selection, navigation and hide/show behaviour. Preserve this where it exists and ensure it does not obstruct core controls.

The exact supplied logo artwork is used in public landing/auth pages and workspace navigation according to the existing README. Preserve it.
15. Technology stack
The existing project documentation identifies this stack:
- Application: Next.js App Router, React, TypeScript.
- Styling: Tailwind CSS plus custom responsive CSS; hybrid light/dark preference.
- Icons: Lucide icons.
- Branding: Supplied BuildBridge logo artwork.
- AI: Google Gemini through @google/genai server-side routes.
- Auth: Supabase Auth using PKCE and cookie-based SSR sessions.
- Database: Supabase PostgreSQL with Row Level Security.
- File storage: Supabase private Storage.
- Email: Resend/outbox integration as documented; Production delivery must be verified.
- Learning resources: YouTube Data API.
- External project discovery: GitHub Search API.
- Hosting: Vercel.
Confirm exact installed package versions and current environment variable names from the actual repository; this README should not be treated as a substitute for package.json or the deployed configuration.
16. Important API and project structure references
The uploaded/original README documents these paths. Always inspect the current working tree before relying on this map, because previous implementation work may have added files or changed locations.
app/
  api/
    ai/route.ts                 # Gemini mentor actions
    backend/route.ts            # Authenticated Supabase actions for all roles
    company-domain/             # DNS proof and admin domain decisions
    cron/notifications/         # Deadline reminders and Resend outbox worker
    notifications/              # User notification centre
    resources/route.ts           # Authenticated YouTube recommendations
    health/route.ts              # Configuration and connectivity status
    external-projects/           # GitHub/external issue discovery (documented)
  auth/                          # Sign-in, sign-up, callback and recovery pages
  workspace/page.tsx             # Authenticated role workspace
  page.tsx                       # Public landing page
  globals.css                    # Light/dark UI system
  layout.tsx                     # Metadata and theme configuration

components/
  WorkspaceApp.tsx               # Authenticated workspace and role navigation
  AccountPreferences.tsx         # Profile/settings components (inspect current role handling)
  CompanyDomainWorkflow.tsx      # Company domain verification workflow
  AuthForm.tsx                   # Authentication form
  SocialAuthButtons.tsx          # Provider sign-in actions (if present)

lib/
  supabase.ts                    # Browser Supabase client
  supabase/server.ts             # Request-scoped server client
  supabase/admin.ts              # Server-only service-role client
  api-auth.ts                    # Auth/role/owner checks (if present)
  types.ts                       # Domain types

supabase/
  provision-admin.sql            # One-deployer admin provisioning
  migrations/                    # Additive SQL migrations

database-schema.sql              # Initial schema/RLS/triggers; not for replay on Production
Other documented files include:
- QUICKSTART-WINDOWS.md
- docs/DATABASE-MIGRATIONS.md
- docs/DEPLOYMENT.md
- BUILD_VALIDATION.md
Inspect the actual source before changing any file or table.
17. Authentication, roles and security rules
- Public sign-up always creates a student profile regardless of signup metadata.
- Company representative selection is onboarding intent only.
- Company access requires confirmed organisational email, DNS ownership proof and separate admin approval.
- Company publishing authorisation is an additional check.
- Admin access is not publicly selectable.
- One trusted admin account is provisioned manually in Supabase.
- The confirmed admin must match ADMIN_OWNER_EMAIL and platform_admin_owner.
- Use server-side checks and RLS for every protected operation.
- Browser/client role state must never be treated as authority.
- Service-role clients must remain server-only.
- Private avatars and Vault files must remain private.
- Public certificate verification must disclose only the intended certificate details.
- Use safe signed URLs for private file access.
- Do not expose student emails, account IDs, private evidence or other personal data in public verification pages.
18. Environment variables and external configuration
The exact names must be checked against current source code and Vercel settings. Do not assume a variable exists just because a similarly named variable exists.

Known configuration references in the project documentation and development history include:
Variable/configuration	Purpose	Notes
NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL or the exact name used by current code	Supabase URL	Confirm canonical name used by browser and server clients.
NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY or NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY	Public Supabase client key	Use the exact supported name in current source.
SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY	Server-only privileged operations	Never expose to browser or commit to Git.
ADMIN_OWNER_EMAIL	Provisioned platform owner email	Must match the single trusted owner record.
GEMINI_API_KEY	Gemini server-side access	Check actual generation, not merely presence.
GEMINI_MODEL	Configured Gemini model	Verify model availability/compatibility.
YOUTUBE_API_KEY	YouTube Data API	Server-side use.
GITHUB_TOKEN	Optional authenticated GitHub API access	Server-only; improves rate limits.
Resend API key/sender configuration	Transactional email	Inspect exact variable names and verified sender.
App base/public URL settings	Callback and email links	Reconcile APP_BASE_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL or current names.


Provider-specific OAuth secrets must be stored in Supabase Auth provider settings or their appropriate secure provider configuration, not committed to Git.
18.1 OAuth provider configuration
Supported intended providers:
- Google.
- GitHub.
- LinkedIn OIDC.
- Microsoft/Azure.
Provider callbacks generally use the Supabase Auth callback URL:

https://<SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF>.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback

BuildBridge's own /auth/callback is the application redirect and should be included in Supabase Auth's allowed redirect URLs. Follow each provider's current official setup and exact Supabase instructions.

LinkedIn OIDC uses the Supabase provider identifier linkedin_oidc and requires the OIDC product/scopes (openid, profile, email) in its provider setup. Microsoft/Azure uses the Supabase provider identifier azure; the app needs the appropriate email scope. Never assume that a provider button means the provider is enabled or configured.
19. Database migrations and Production safety
See docs/DATABASE-MIGRATIONS.md before database operations.

The existing migration guide distinguishes a fresh Supabase installation from an existing BuildBridge database and starts existing-database work with read-only preflight SQL.

Do not run database-schema.sql against Production.
Do not replay the older supabase/project-marketplace.sql after the versioned project workflow migration.

The original README's "Latest additive migration" section points to 20261121050000_profile_and_auth_enhancements.sql, but other development notes refer to later migrations (including a 20261121057000 migration). Treat the old README migration statement as potentially stale. Inspect the actual supabase/migrations/ directory and migration history before applying any migration.
19.1 Required preflight/postflight habits
Before an additive migration:
1. Confirm the correct Supabase project/ref.
2. Read current table/column definitions.
3. Check foreign keys, indexes and constraints.
4. Check existing policies and triggers.
5. Check the migration history.
6. Confirm the migration has not already been applied.
7. Back up or otherwise protect existing data where appropriate.
8. Review SQL before execution.
After a migration:
- Verify actual table/column existence.
- Verify RLS and Storage policies.
- Verify triggers/functions.
- Verify PostgREST schema visibility.
- Test the related authenticated API.
19.2 Known Vault schema-cache error
Production returned:

Could not find the table 'public.student_vault_artifacts' in the schema cache

Check with read-only SQL:
SELECT to_regclass('public.student_vault_artifacts') AS vault_table;
If it returns NULL, inspect and apply the correct existing Vault migration to the correct Production database. If the table exists, a PostgREST cache reload may be appropriate:
NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema';
Do not guess the Vault table schema or create an improvised duplicate. Verify its relationships, RLS and Storage policies from the generated migration.
19.3 Known project/coursework schema history
Previous troubleshooting documented missing or inconsistent coursework/project tables and project milestone columns. Some later reports contradicted earlier snapshots, so do not assume the old report is the current schema. Run the current read-only preflight against the exact Production project and reconcile its output with migrations before making changes.
20. Known implementation status and unresolved issues
This section is especially important for future development sessions. It distinguishes what has been reported working from what remains broken or unverified. Status is based on owner observations and development-session evidence, not on assumptions from UI presence or successful builds.

Status vocabulary:
- Reported working: The application owner personally observed the workflow working at least once.
- Code/build reported: A previous implementation task reported code changes/build checks, but Production behaviour has not been confirmed.
- Unresolved: A current error or failed workflow has been observed.
- Needs verification: The feature may exist, but end-to-end Production operation has not been demonstrated.
- Planned: Product requirement agreed, implementation not yet confirmed.
Area	Current known status	Evidence / next action
Student core workflow	Reported working	Owner says the student workflow works. Preserve it and perform regression tests only.
Admin login via GitHub	Reported working at least once	Admin Console opened using GitHub. First-attempt consistency still needs testing.
LinkedIn OIDC login	Reported working at least once	Provider was enabled/configured and owner reported successful login. Test first-attempt and subsequent login.
Google login	Unresolved	Requires two attempts; earlier callback error flow_state_already_used. Inspect PKCE/callback/session lifecycle and account identity linking.
GitHub login	Needs verification	Admin access succeeded, but two-attempt behaviour was reported. Confirm first-attempt consistency.
Microsoft/Azure login	Unresolved	UI displayed Unable to exchange external code: M.C5. Entra app exists, supports all Microsoft account users, and has one Web redirect URI/one secret; exact redirect, secret validity/value and Supabase Azure settings still need verification.
Company domain requests	Unresolved	PostgREST ambiguity between profiles requester and reviewer FKs. Use explicit company_domain_requests_user_id_fkey and company_domain_requests_reviewed_by_fkey joins.
Company Profile/Settings separation	Prompt given; completion unverified	Screenshots showed company users receiving student education/profile and learning notification fields. Inspect current code for completed role-specific components before repeating changes.
Company email/domain gating	Needs verification	Confirmed organization email, domain ownership proof and admin approval must be enforced before company publishing/approval access.
Practice project draft saving	Unresolved	Save Draft appeared to do nothing, record list remained empty and data disappeared after leaving. Trace POST/DB/RLS/GET/filtering.
AI Mentor	Needs repair/verification	It returned a generic SQL explanation that assumed MySQL/Node.js and did not use apparent student context. Verify live Gemini call, context retrieval, system prompt and conversation persistence.
Admin AI Management	Incomplete/placeholder observed	Displayed generic empty state. Needs genuine provider health, generation tests and database-backed operational telemetry.
Audit Trail	Incomplete/empty observed	Displayed no events. Existing endpoint reads audit_logs; event capture is limited. Implement centralised event recording, safe metadata, filters and RLS.
Admin Settings	Incomplete/placeholder observed	Displayed generic empty state. Needs administrator preferences and operational alert settings.
Users & Access	Needs UI/workflow improvement	Horizontal overflow clipped role badges; add company Review Verification shortcut to existing request.
Company verification review UI	Unresolved	Request list fails due to ambiguous profiles relationship.
Company completion certificates	Needs end-to-end verification	Design says automatic after all required milestones are approved by assigning verified company and authorised named signatory. Verify trigger, Vault, notification and actual email.
Admin platform certificates	Separate valid workflow	Admin manual credential issuance is for BuildBridge learning/skill credentials, not company completion certificates. Preserve the distinction.
Transactional email	Needs verification	Owner reports receiving auth emails only; application-triggered email delivery is not proven. Inspect existing Resend/outbox/cron.
Unified Student Vault	Code/build reported; Production DB unresolved	Previous Copilot reported implementation and passing typecheck/lint/build. Production table missing from PostgREST schema cache: public.student_vault_artifacts. Apply/verify correct migration.
Open-source skill matching	Partly documented; enhancement planned	Existing GitHub issue discovery is documented. Verify student-specific matching/reasoning.
AI issue simplification + Show Details	Planned/needs verification	Must explain unfamiliar terms in plain language while preserving original GitHub issue details.
Raw GitHub issue HTML/Markdown	Issue observed	Screenshot showed raw HTML/Markdown in issue description. Implement safe rendering/sanitisation.
Landing page	Needs refinement	Owner wants a clearer first-sentence product proposition and removal/fixing of redundant/non-functional CTAs.
Sign-out destination	Needs correction/verification	Original code redirected to /; intended destination is /auth/sign-in. Verify current working tree.
README	Being expanded	This README is the comprehensive developer handoff; later create a shorter judge-optimised public version.


20.1 OAuth provider configuration evidence
- LinkedIn: The LinkedIn Developer app screenshot showed "Sign In with LinkedIn using OpenID Connect" added. The owner later reported LinkedIn login worked. Supabase provider activation, linkedin_oidc, client credentials and exact callback must remain documented.
- Microsoft: Microsoft Entra application showed an activated app, all Microsoft account users supported, one Web redirect URI and one client secret. The sign-in failure was Unable to exchange external code: M.C5. This is not enough evidence to claim the redirect or secret is the exact cause. Inspect the configured redirect URI, secret expiry/value, tenant URL and Supabase provider settings.
- GitHub: The owner reached the Admin Console using GitHub. Do not change a working callback without checking its current configuration.
- Google: flow_state_already_used was previously observed. Do not retry an already-consumed authorization code. Investigate multiple callback handling, PKCE verifier storage, cookie handling and domain consistency.
20.2 Important distinction: error vs missing implementation
A message such as "No records available" can mean there are no records, but it can also mean the section is a generic placeholder with no dedicated data workflow. Inspect the component, endpoint and database query before classifying it as a data problem. Similarly, an API health response that says Supabase is reachable does not prove that privileged operations, Storage, email, Gemini generation or all application tables work.
21. Visual identity and UI conventions
The existing product design language is:
- Light-peach application canvas.
- Warm ivory/white cards.
- Deep navy text and navigation.
- Emerald/mint primary actions.
- Violet secondary accents.
- Rounded panels, subtle borders and shadows.
- Lucide icons.
- Supplied BuildBridge logo artwork.
- Hybrid light/dark appearance preference.
Keep layouts responsive and readable. Ensure that no important text, role badge, table column or action button is clipped. Avoid unnecessary horizontal scrolling. Keep role-specific pages visually consistent but functionally distinct.

Do not redesign functional student experiences just for visual novelty.
22. Local development and deployment
Requirements
The existing README specifies Node.js 22+.

On Windows PowerShell, use npm.cmd if script execution policy blocks npm.ps1.
Install
npm.cmd ci
Environment
Create a local .env.local using the exact names required by the current source. Never commit .env, .env.local, API keys, OAuth secrets, service-role keys or other credentials.
Run locally
Inspect package.json for current scripts. The expected Next.js development command is typically:
npm.cmd run dev
Validation
Run the scripts available in the current package.json, including where present:
npm.cmd run typecheck
npm.cmd run lint
npm.cmd run build
The previous Vault implementation task reported typecheck and build passing and lint passing with existing warnings. Treat that as a historical result for that change, not proof that the current full branch or Production application passes.
Vercel deployment
- Confirm the GitHub repository connected to the Vercel project.
- Confirm the Production branch.
- Confirm the canonical Production domain.
- Confirm environment variables are set for Production, not only Preview.
- Redeploy after changing environment variables.
- Verify public access in an incognito browser.
- Test the actual latest deployment, not a stale preview URL.
Do not assume a successful Vercel build means Supabase migrations were applied.
23. Git workflow and release safety
Before making changes:
git status
git branch --show-current
git diff --check
git diff --stat
After implementation, inspect all diffs and ensure no secrets or generated sensitive files are staged.

Typical checkpoint workflow:
git add .
git commit -m "Describe the actual changes"
git push origin main
Do not use git push --force on the shared/main branch. Do not push unreviewed migrations or secrets. Commit meaningful checkpoints so completed work can be recovered.
24. Development priorities
When continuing development, prioritise in this order. Check the current working tree before acting because some items may have been fixed since this document was written.
P0 — Critical production workflows
1. Restore reliable first-attempt OAuth and session handling for configured providers.
2. Fix company-domain request loading by explicitly identifying the requester/reviewer foreign keys.
3. Fix practice-project draft insertion, listing, persistence and editing.
4. Apply/verify the Unified Vault migration and its RLS/Storage policies in the correct Production Supabase project.
5. Verify company email/domain verification and administrator approval gates.
6. Make the AI Mentor perform real, context-aware Gemini generation and persist conversations.
7. Verify company project milestone approval and automatic company completion certificate issuance.
8. Verify actual transactional email delivery.
P1 — Complete the Admin Console
1. AI Management with real telemetry and provider tests.
2. Audit Trail with centralised event capture.
3. Functional Admin Settings and operational alert preferences.
4. Users & Access responsive UI and company verification shortcut.
5. Correct company/administrator Profile and Settings separation.
P1 — Core product integration
1. Complete the Student Vault connections to resources, projects, evidence, reports and certificates.
2. Connect skills and learning records to project recommendations.
3. Complete skill-based open-source matching and AI explanation.
4. Safely render GitHub issue descriptions and add Show details.
5. Ensure opportunity matching uses demonstrated evidence and eligibility.
P2 — Product communication and polish
1. Improve the landing-page first-screen proposition.
2. Fix or remove non-functional CTAs.
3. Update README and architecture documentation.
4. Add real screenshots and a short recorded demo.
5. Prepare a concise judge-optimised README and pitch narrative after implementation is verified.
25. Hackathon context and evaluation alignment
BuildBridge was developed for Prompt War – 24-Hour AI Hackathon, organised by AI Geeks Chennai.

The published themes included:
1. Automation — Intelligent AI Agents.
2. AI for Education.
3. AI for Sports.
4. AI for Finance.
5. AI for Healthcare.
6. Open Innovation.
Recommended submission theme: AI for Education. Open Innovation is also relevant, but the primary positioning should remain AI for Education.

The announced judging criteria were:
- Problem Statement Alignment.
- Feasibility.
- Scalability.
- Impact.
Mandatory submission materials described in the event announcements:
- Public GitHub repository with source code and README.
- Working deployed prototype.
- Clear project description with problem, solution, features and technologies/AI models.
- LinkedIn post describing the project and tagging AI Geeks Chennai and the Hack2skill team, where required by the final submission instructions.
The organisers explicitly stated that judges would review the actual idea, implementation and working prototype, not AI-generated evaluation scores.
25.1 Recommended BuildBridge judging story
Problem: Traditional learning often stops at watching videos, completing courses and collecting course-completion certificates. Students still struggle to apply what they learned, build real projects, gain credible experience and prove their capabilities to employers. Learning resources, practical work and opportunities are also scattered across disconnected platforms.

Solution: BuildBridge bridges traditional learning to real-world work. It organises existing resources by skills and goals, guides students through practical projects and open-source contributions, preserves their work and evidence in one place, and connects demonstrated capabilities to verified company assignments and relevant professional opportunities.

Innovation: A unified, evidence-backed learning-to-work record and a progression from studied to practised, demonstrated and company-verified skills.

Feasibility: Demonstrate actual working student learning, project evidence, company review and opportunity workflows. Do not claim a workflow works unless it has been tested.

Impact: Reduce resource fragmentation, help students build confidence through visible progress and proof of work, and improve access to relevant professional opportunities.

For judge-facing presentation, demonstrate one coherent student-to-company journey rather than listing every feature.
26. Guidance for future AI/developer sessions
When another developer or AI assistant continues BuildBridge:
1. Read this README first.
2. Inspect the latest repository state and git status.
3. Check current Production behaviour before trusting the status table.
4. Preserve the working student workflow.
5. Do not confuse planned requirements with implemented features.
6. Do not repeat changes already made in the current branch.
7. Inspect actual database migrations and current schema before writing SQL.
8. Never reset or recreate Production data.
9. Never expose credentials or private student records.
10. Keep company verification separate from company certificate authorisation.
11. Keep practice project progress separate from company-approved milestones.
12. Keep company completion certificates separate from administrator-issued platform credentials.
13. Treat external GitHub issue content as untrusted input.
14. Treat health-check configuration presence as different from a real functional test.
15. Report statuses honestly: implemented, tested, partially implemented, blocked by external configuration or not implemented.
16. After changes, report files modified, migrations, environment variables, actual test results and required manual Production steps.
Final product principle
BuildBridge should make the entire journey from learning to demonstrable professional capability coherent, connected and accessible.

A student should be able to discover the right existing resources, understand what to learn next, keep a record of learning, build real things, collect credible proof, earn appropriate credentials and discover relevant opportunities—all from one connected platform.

BuildBridge — Bridging traditional learning to real-world work.
